I. 8. 61, 65-66 ; compare योगयाज्ञवल्क्य (ed. by Mr. Diwanji) IV. 64-71 for the ten vāyus and their functions; वनपर्व 213. 16 (वहन्त्यक्षरमान् नाड्यो दशप्राणप्रचोदिताः) mentions the function of ten prāṇas. Vide Dr. B. N. Seal's 'the positive sciences of the ancient Hindus' (Longmans, Green, 1915) pp. 228-231 for explanation about these ten.

[[P1437]]
It is now proper to see how the [[Y, S,|Y. S.]] defines and explains [[prāṇāyama|prāṇāyāma]]. 'When the stability of posture has been assured (or secured) prāṇāyāma is the pause (lit. cutting off) in the movements of inhalation and exhalation'. The bhāṣya explains '[[śvāss'|śvāsa']] as 'drawing in of the air which is outside one's body' and '[[praśyāsa'|praśvāsa']] as the expelling of the air in the trunk (or chest)' and the absence of both these is prāṇāyāma. This shows that the chief element in prāṇāyāma is the absence of both inhalation and exhalation i. [[e,|e.]] kumbhaka in the technical language of yoga works. The next sūtra states that prāṇāyāma (the pause in movement) is of three kinds, viz. external, internal and suppressed. The idea is that Kumbhaka (stopping or pause) may be done after one has taken in air from outside (first kind) or after one has expelled air from the trunk (2nd kind) or when one is in a normal condition (i. e. [[neitber|neither]] making an effort to fill his chest with air or emptying it of air) one may make a pause (3rd kind). Each of these may be regulated and considered from the point of the units of space, time (measurement by kalās or [[mātrūs)|mātrās)]] and numbers. Prāṇāyāma is called mṛdu (mild) when pause is kept up for 36 mātrās, then moderate one when [[kopt|kept]] up for 72 mātrās and keen ([[tivra|tīvra]]) when kept up for 108 [[matrās|mātrās]]. When practised for days, fortnights and months, it is called protracted and when performed with great skill it is called sūkṣma (subtle).

In connection with prāṇāyāma we must look to Y. S. I. 34 also. That sūtra lays down that for securing the undisturbed calm of the mind one remedy is the expulsion and retention of the breath.[^2360] It appears from that sūtra and its bhāṣya that [[vidhārana|vidhāraṇa]] (retention of breath, [[I.|i.]] e. Kumbhaka) is prāṇāyāma.

Some explanation must be offered about [[desa|deśa]] (space), kāla and [[sankhyā|saṅkhyā]] in relation to prāṇāyāma. Ordinarily a healthy grown-up man breathes in and out in about four seconds [[i,|i.]] e. about 15 times a minute (21600 times in one day and night). In order to measure the force of the recaka a piece of cotton or a thin thread is held at some distance from the nostrils and the distance up to which the air driven from the nostrils makes the cotton or thread move or cease to move is measured by means of finger-breadths. As regards time several units of time are mentioned because in those ancient days there was no scientific

[^2360]: [[प्रच्छर्दन-विधारणाभ्यां वा प्राणस्य । यो. सू. I. 34; कोष्ठ्यस्य वायोर्नासिका- पुटाभ्यां प्रयत्नविशेषाद्वमनं प्रच्छर्दनं विधारणं प्राणायामस्ताभ्यां वा मनसः स्थितिं सम्पादयेत् । भाष्य.|प्रच्छर्दन-विधारणाभ्यां वा प्राणस्य । यो. सू. I. 34; कोष्ठ्यस्य वायोर्नासिकापुटाभ्यां प्रयत्नविशेषाद्वमनं प्रच्छर्दनं विधारणं प्राणायामस्ताभ्यां वा मनसः स्थितिं सम्पादयेत् । भाष्य.]]

[[P1438]]
apparatus. The time taken for winking ([[nimesa|nimeṣa]]) is equal[^2361] to the time required for pronouncing [[&|a]] short vowel and this was called [[mulrū|mātrā]]. The time taken for touching one's [[kneos|knees]] thrice with one's hand and snapping the thumb and forefinger was also called [[mnātrā|mātrā]]. Other units are passed over. The general rules are that both [[recuka|recaka]] and [[pīraka|pūraka]] must be uniform and quiet, that the time given for pūraka should be half of that given to recaka. Three views about the duration of pūraka, kumbhaka and recaka are that they should be in the proportion of 1:4:2 or 1:2:2 or the same for all. The Purāṇas give different [[nātrās|mātrās]] for [[prapāyāmas|prāṇāyāmas]] e. g. the [[Mārkandeya|Mārkaṇḍeya]] (36. 13, 14) says that the laghu (mṛdu of the [[bhāsya|bhāṣya]]) is of twelve mātrās, double of that [[ia|is]] madhyama and the [[uttariya|uttarīya]] ([[tivra|tīvra]] in the [[bhāsya|bhāṣya]]) is for three times twelve mātrās, while the [[Garudapurāna|Garuḍapurāṇa]] (I. 226. 14-15) puts down the figures as 10, 20, 30 respectively and the [[Kūrma purāna|Kūrmapurāṇa]] (II. 11. 32) agrees with the [[Mārkapdeya|Mārkaṇḍeya]]. The [[Mitaksara|Mitākṣarā]] on Yāj. III. 200-201 provides that prāṇāyāma is of three varieties (adhama of 15 mātrās, madhyama of 30 mātrās and uttama of 45 mātrās). The [[Lingapurāna|Liṅgapurāṇa]] (I. 8. 47-48) also puts forth 12, 24, 36 mātrās as the time of [[nici unghała|nīca udghāta]], madhyama udghāta and mukhya and states that obvious results of these three are respectively perspiration, [[treinor|tremor]] and utthāna ([[prasāda-kampa. notthāna-janakaśca|prasāda-kampanotthāna-janakaśca]] yathākramam). Compare Mārk. 36. 16 which states that [[those|these]] three are to be mastered respectively by the different lengths of prāṇāyāma ([[prathamena jayet svedam madhyamena ca repathuma / viṇādam hi tftiyena jayed-doṣan anukramāt 11|prathamena jayet svedaṁ madhyamena ca vepathum । viṣādaṁ hi tṛtīyena jayed-doṣān anukramāt ॥]]).

It should be noticed that neither Patañjali nor the Vyāsa-bhāṣya employs the well-known words, [[piruka|pūraka]], recaka and

[^2361]: [[निमेषकालतुल्यं हि विद्याल्लघ्वक्षरं च यत् । वायुपु. 57. 6; निमेषो मानुषो योऽयं मात्रामात्रप्रमाणतः । ब्रह्मा 231. 6; निमेषोन्मेषणा मात्रा तालो लघ्वक्षरं तथा ॥ मार्क० 36. 13 (Venk. ed.). विष्णुधर्मोत्तर I. 73. 1 लघ्वक्षरसमा मात्रा निमेषः; परिकीर्तितः । वाचस्पति says 'स्वजानुमण्डलं पाणिना त्रिः परामृश्य च्छोटिकावच्छिन्नः कालो मात्रा ताभिः षट्त्रिंशता मात्राभिः परिमितः प्रथमः उद्घातो मृदुः । स एव द्विगुणीकृतो द्वितीयो मध्यमः । स एव त्रिगुणी- कृतस्तृतीयस्तीव्रः ।' on यो. सू. II. 50; अङ्गुलिमोक्षत्रितयं जान्वोः परिमार्जनमथापि । तत्कालत्रयमपि तज्ज्ञा मात्रासंज्ञां प्रशंसन्ति ॥ बृहद्योगिया. VIII. 12 q. as योगियाज्ञवल्क्य in कृत्यकल्प० (मोक्षकाण्ड p. 171). The गोरक्षशतक says 'प्राणायामो भवेत्त्रेधा मात्रा- द्वादशसंयुतः ॥ द्वादशाधमके मात्रा मध्यमे द्विगुणस्ततः । उत्तमे त्रिगुणा मात्राः प्राणायामस्य निर्णयः ॥ verses 47-48; vide वायुपु. 11. 80-81 मन्दो द्वादशमात्रस्तु उद्घाता द्वादश स्मृताः । मध्यमश्च द्विरुद्धातश्चतुर्विंशतिमात्रिकः ॥ उत्तमः स्यात् त्रिरुद्धातो मात्राः षट्त्रिंशदुच्यते ॥. The योगतत्त्वोपनिषद् (verses 41-42) lays down the proportion of the three (पूरक, कुम्भक and रेचक) is as 16, 64, 32 mātrās, while the गरुडपु. (I. 226. 14-15) speaks of 10, 20, 30 mātrās.|निमेषकालतुल्यं हि विद्याल्लघ्वक्षरं च यत् । वायुपु. 57. 6; निमेषो मानुषो योऽयं मात्रामात्रप्रमाणतः । ब्रह्मा 231. 6; निमेषोन्मेषणा मात्रा तालो लघ्वक्षरं तथा ॥ मार्क० 36. 13 (Venk. ed.). विष्णुधर्मोत्तर I. 73. 1 लघ्वक्षरसमा मात्रा निमेषः; परिकीर्तितः । वाचस्पति says 'स्वजानुमण्डलं पाणिना त्रिः परामृश्य च्छोटिकावच्छिन्नः कालो मात्रा ताभिः षट्त्रिंशता मात्राभिः परिमितः प्रथमः उद्घातो मृदुः । स एव द्विगुणीकृतो द्वितीयो मध्यमः । स एव त्रिगुणीकृतस्तृतीयस्तीव्रः ।' on यो. सू. II. 50; अङ्गुलिमोक्षत्रितयं जान्वोः परिमार्जनमथापि । तत्कालत्रयमपि तज्ज्ञा मात्रासंज्ञां प्रशंसन्ति ॥ बृहद्योगिया. VIII. 12 q. as योगियाज्ञवल्क्य in कृत्यकल्प० (मोक्षकाण्ड p. 171). The गोरक्षशतक says 'प्राणायामो भवेत्त्रेधा मात्राद्वादशसंयुतः ॥ द्वादशाधमके मात्रा मध्यमे द्विगुणस्ततः । उत्तमे त्रिगुणा मात्राः प्राणायामस्य निर्णयः ॥ verses 47-48; vide वायुपु. 11. 80-81 मन्दो द्वादशमात्रस्तु उद्घाता द्वादश स्मृताः । मध्यमश्च द्विरुद्धातश्चतुर्विंशतिमात्रिकः ॥ उत्तमः स्यात् त्रिरुद्धातो मात्राः षट्त्रिंशदुच्यते ॥. The योगतत्त्वोपनिषद् (verses 41-42) lays down the proportion of the three (पूरक, कुम्भक and रेचक) is as 16, 64, 32 mātrās, while the गरुडपु. (I. 226. 14-15) speaks of 10, 20, 30 mātrās.]]

[[P1439]]
kumbhaka, but only śvāsa, [[praśyāga|praśvāsa]] and gativiccheda.[^2362] Besides, Patañjali and Vyāsa say nothing about the japa of Om, [[Gāyatri|Gāyatrī]] or [[Vyābśtis|Vyāhṛtis]] in prāṇāyāma, as the smṛtis and later medieval works do. A third matter to be considered is that in some later works [[rocaka,|recaka,]] pūraka and kumbhaka are said to be three prāṇāyāmas and this latter is said to be of four kinds, three set out in Y. S. II. 50 and the fourth in [[II, 51.|II. 51.]]

The words 'recaka', 'pūraka' and 'kumbhaka' also must be, however, regarded as ancient enough. They are mentioned and defined by the Dharmasūtra of [[Dovala|Devala]][^2363] referred to by

[^2362]: [[तस्मिन्सति श्वासप्रश्वासयोर्गतिविच्छेदः प्राणायामः । बाह्याग्यन्तरस्तम्भ वृत्तिदेशकालसंख्याभिः परिदृष्टो दीर्घसूक्ष्मः । बाह्याभ्यन्तरविषयाक्षेपी चतुर्थः । यो. सू. II. 49-51; सत्यासनजये बाह्यस्य वायोराचमनं श्वासः कोष्ठ्यस्य वायोनिःसारण प्रवासः तयोर्गतिविच्छेद: उभयाभावः प्राणायामः। भाष्य on II. 49. The word वृत्ति is to be connected with each of the three words बाह्य, आभ्यन्तर and स्तम्भ. Here the कुम्भक that follows रेचक is called बारावृत्ति and the one that comes after पूरक is called आभ्यन्तरवृत्ति. The कुम्भक कि made when there is neither रेचक nor पूरक is called स्तम्भवृत्ति. For an exhaustive and lucid exposition of the Pāla õjala sūtras on prānāyāma, tbe bhāsya thereon and the views of otber commentators, vide Yogamimamsa (of Shri Kuvalayananda) vol. VI. pp. 44 54, 129-145,225-257.|तस्मिन्सति श्वासप्रश्वासयोर्गतिविच्छेदः प्राणायामः । बाह्याभ्यन्तरस्तम्भवृत्तिर्देशकालसंख्याभिः परिदृष्टो दीर्घसूक्ष्मः । बाह्याभ्यन्तरविषयाक्षेपी चतुर्थः । यो. सू. II. 49-51; सत्यासनजये बाह्यस्य वायोराचमनं श्वासः कोष्ठ्यस्य वायोर्निःसारणं प्रश्वासः तयोर्गतिविच्छेदः उभयाभावः प्राणायामः । भाष्य on II. 49. The word वृत्ति is to be connected with each of the three words बाह्य, आभ्यन्तर and स्तम्भ. Here the कुम्भक that follows रेचक is called बाह्यवृत्ति and the one that comes after पूरक is called आभ्यन्तरवृत्ति. The कुम्भक that is made when there is neither रेचक nor पूरक is called स्तम्भवृत्ति. For an exhaustive and lucid exposition of the Pātañjala sūtras on prāṇāyāma, the bhāṣya thereon and the views of other commentators, vide Yogamīmāṁsā (of Shri Kuvalayananda) vol. VI. pp. 44-54, 129-145, 225-257.]]
[^2363]: [[वेवलः । त्रिविधः प्राणायामः। कुम्भो रेचन पूरणमिति। निश्वासनिरोधः कुम्भः । अजननिःश्वासो रेचनम्। निश्वासामानं पूरणमिति । स पुनरेकद्वित्रिभिरुद्वातै- (याते।) संदर्मन्दस्तीक्ष्णो वा भवति। प्राणापानण्यानोदानसमानान सकमनं मूर्धानमाहत्य (हत्य!) निवृत्तिश्वोद्वातः (धातः)। कृत्यकल्प (मोक्षकाण्ड p. 170) and अपराक p. 1023. Compare व्यासभाव्य 'संख्याभिः परिवष्टा एतापद्धिः श्वासप्रश्वासैः प्रथम उद्धातस्तद्वनि गृहीतस्यैतावद्धिद्वितीय उद्यातः। एवं तृतीयः। एवं मृदुरेवं मध्य एवं तीव इति संख्या परिदृष्टः' on यो. स. II. 50. The राजमार्तण्ड explains 'उद्घातो नाम नाभिमूला मेरितस्य पायोः शिरस्यभिहननम्।. This word is differently explained by different writers: vide योगमीमांसा vol. II. part 3 pp. 225-234. Sometimes पूरक, रेचक and कुम्भक are said to be three माणायाम, sometimes all three together are deemed to be one प्राणायाम. Each of these again is either भूद, मन्द (or मध्यम) and तीन. vide बृहयोगिया. VIII. 7 त्रिविध केचिदिच्छन्ति तथा च नवधा परे। मुद्ध मध्याधिमात्रत्वादेकैक विविधं भवेत् .. Vide विष्णुधर्मोत्तर III. 280. 1 रेचक पूरक व कुम्भकं च तथा द्विजाः। एकरूयवस्थो विज्ञेयः प्राणायामो महाफला रेचक पूरक कुम्भके प्वस्ति श्वास-प्रश्वासयोगतिविच्छेद इति प्राणायामसामान्यलक्षणमेतदिति । तथाहि । पत्र पायो पायुराचम्यान्तर्धार्यते पूरके तनास्ति श्वास-प्रश्वासयोगतिविच्छेदः। यत्रापि कौठो वायुविरेच्य पहिायते तत्रास्ति श्वासप्रश्वासयोगतिविच्छेदः। एवं कुम्भकपीति। वाचस्पति on यो.सू. II. 50% पूरकः कुम्भकश्चैव रेचकस्तदनन्तरम् । प्राणायामनिधा ज्ञेयः कनीयों मध्यमोत्तमः। पूरकः कुम्भको रेच्या प्राणायामनिलक्षणः। वृहयोगिया. VIII. 9-10; the last half verse is quoted by स्मृतिच, I. p. 142 (as योगया.) and the three are defined in वृहयोगिया. VIII. 19-21 of which verse 19 isq.by स्मृतिच. I. p.142. कुम्भक is so called because in it there is resemblance to a jar filled with water (which remains steady in it). राजमार्तण्ड explains 'तस्मिालमिव कुम्भे निचलतया पापा अवस्थाप्यन्ते इति कुम्भकः. vide पाणिनि V.3.97 'प्रतिकृती था, इवा कर स्वाद समदायेन संज्ञा गम्यते ।. So कुम्भक means कुम्भ इव कुम्भका कुम्भसहसव संशा.|देवलः । त्रिविधः प्राणायामः । कुम्भो रेचनं पूरणमिति । निःश्वासनिरोधः कुम्भः । अजस्रनिःश्वासो रेचनम् । निःश्वासाध्मानं पूरणमिति । स पुनरेकद्वित्रिभिरुद्घातैर्मृदुर्मन्दस्तीक्ष्णो वा भवति । प्राणापानव्यानोदानसमानान् सकृन्मूर्धानमाहत्य निवृत्तिश्चोद्घातः । कृत्यकल्प० (मोक्षकाण्ड p. 170) and अपरार्क p. 1023. Compare व्यासभाष्य 'संख्याभिः परिदृष्टः एतावद्भिः श्वासप्रश्वासैः प्रथम उद्धातस्तद्वन्निगृहीतस्यैतावद्भिर्द्वितीय उद्घातः । एवं तृतीयः । एवं मृदुरेवं मध्य एवं तीव्र इति संख्यापरिदृष्टः ' on यो. सू. II. 50. The राजमार्तण्ड explains 'उद्घातो नाम नाभिमूलात्प्रेरितस्य वायोः शिरस्यभिहननम्'. This word is differently explained by different writers: vide योगमीमांसा vol. II. part 3 pp. 225-234. Sometimes पूरक, रेचक and कुम्भक are said to be three प्राणायामs, sometimes all three together are deemed to be one प्राणायाम. Each of these again is either मृदु, मन्द (or मध्यम) and तीव्र. vide बृहद्योगिया. VIII. 7 त्रिविधं केचिदिच्छन्ति तथा च नवधा परे । मृदुमध्याधिमात्रत्वादेकैकं त्रिविधं भवेत् ॥. Vide विष्णुधर्मोत्तर III. 280. 1 रेचकं पूरकं चैव कुम्भकं च तथा द्विजाः । एकैकव्यवस्था विज्ञेया प्राणायामो महाफलः ॥ रेचकपूरककुम्भकेष्वस्ति श्वासप्रश्वासयोर्गतिविच्छेद इति प्राणायामसामान्यलक्षणमेतदिति । तथाहि । यत्र बाह्यो वायुराचम्यान्तर्धार्यते पूरके तत्रास्ति श्वासप्रश्वासयोर्गतिविच्छेदः । यत्रापि कौष्ठ्यो वायुर्विरेच्य बहिर्धार्यते तत्रास्ति श्वासप्रश्वासयोर्गतिविच्छेदः । एवं कुम्भकेऽपीति । वाचस्पति on यो. सू. II. 50; पूरकः कुम्भकश्चैव रेचकस्तदनन्तरम् । प्राणायामस्त्रिधा ज्ञेयः कनीयान्मध्यमोत्तमः ॥ पूरकः कुम्भको रेच्यः प्राणायामस्त्रिलक्षणः । बृहद्योगिया. VIII. 9-10; the last half verse is quoted by स्मृतिच. I. p. 142 (as योगया.) and the three are defined in बृहद्योगिया. VIII. 19-21 of which verse 19 is q. by स्मृतिच. I. p. 142. कुम्भक is so called because in it there is resemblance to a jar filled with water (which remains steady in it). राजमार्तण्ड explains 'तस्मिञ्जलमिव कुम्भे निश्चलतया प्राणा अवस्थाप्यन्ते इति कुम्भकः'. vide पाणिनि V. 3. 96 'प्रतिकृतौ' इवे प्रतिकृतौ स्वाङ्गे समुदायेन संज्ञा गम्यते ।. So कुम्भक means कुम्भ इव कुम्भकः कुम्भसदृशस्य संज्ञा.]]

[[P1440]]
Śaṅkarācārya (vide note 2208 above). The [[Bphad-Yogi-Yaj,|Bṛhad-Yogi-Yāj.]] and [[Vacaspati|Vācaspati]] also mention them. The Viṣṇupurāṇa in a poetic description of the [[Sarad|Śarad]] season employs them by way of [[sleṣa|śleṣa]][^2364] (as below). Several modes of performing [[prāṣāyāma|prāṇāyāma]] are prescribed. One of the simplest is to stop the right nostril with the thumb, to fill in air through the left nostril according to one's ability; then to throw out the air through the right nostril; again inhale through the right nostril and [[ejact|eject]] air through the left. Do this at least thrice. Practise this at least twice every day, preferably in the morning after bath and in the evening or four times (before dawn, during midday, in the evening or at midnight). In the beginning kumbhaka need not be practised at all. After some practice with pūraka and recaka, [[kumbbaka|kumbhaka]] may be begun after recaka. Kumbhaka after pūraka is to be practised with great caution and should be attempted under the direction of an expert.

The Manusmṛti contains[^2365] a great eulogy of [[prāṇīyāma|prāṇāyāma]] in the following words 'even [[threo|three]] [[prānāyāmas|prāṇāyāmas]] performed according to the [[rulcs|rules]] prescribed and accompanied by the [[vyābritis|vyāhṛtis]] and [[pruṇaru|praṇava]] (the syllable om) should be regarded as the highest tapas for a brāhmaṇa; just as in the case of metals (like gold and silver) impurities are burnt when they are melted in the blast of a furnace, so blemishes (like passion &c.) of the organs of sense (including the mind) are destroyed by the control of breath; one should extinguish the blemishes (passion, hatred &c.) by prāṇāyāmas, sin by [[dhāranū,|dhāraṇā,]] contact with the objects of sense by [[pratyāhūra|pratyāhāra]] and such attributes as are ungodly (viz. anger, avarice, jealousy &c.) by meditation (on brahma). The Y. S. states that from the practice of prāṇāyāma results the dwindling of actions (or kleśas) that envelop enlightenment (which is the characteristic of [[saltva)|sattva)]] and the

[^2364]: [[प्राणायाम इवाम्भोभिः सरसां कतपूरकैः । अभ्यस्यतेऽनदिवर्स रेचकाकुम्भका pafu: 1 nagy. V. 10, 14,|प्राणायाम इवाम्भोभिः सरसां कृतपूरकैः । अभ्यस्यतेऽनिशमह्नां रेचकाकुम्भकादिभिः ॥ विष्णुपु. V. 10. 14.]]

All these are meantirir. VIII, 29, 30, 32. I. ed.; it is quoted from मार्कण्डेय by कृत्यकल्प० (मोक्षकाण्ड p. 168), which explains 'दोषान् स्वेदवेपथुप्रभृतीन्, अनीश्वरान्, प्राकृतान्'. अपरार्क p. 990 explains 'अनीश्वरा गुणा रागद्वेषादयः'.]]

[[P1441]]mind of the yogin becomes fit for fixed attention.[^2366] The Gorakṣaśataka[^2367] provides 'a yogin always eradicates diseases by [[Zsana|āsana]], sin by [[prāṣāyāmas|prāṇāyāmas]] and mental disturbance by pratyāhāra'. The Smṛtis state that [[prāṣāyāmas|prāṇāyāmas]] have great efficacy in destroying sins. [[Madu|Manu]] XI. 248 ( = Vasiṣṭha 26. 4, Baud. Dh. [[8.|S.]] IV. [[1, 31|1. 31]] and [[Saṅkbasmrti|Śaṅkhasmṛti]] XII. 18-19) states 'sixteen prāṇāyāmas performed everyday for a month together with the [[vāhrtis|vyāhṛtis]] and praṇava (om) purify even the murderer of a brāhmaṇa. Manu XI. 199 and 201 prescribe the performance of a single prāṇāyāma as a purification for light lapses or riding an ass or a camel and such incidents as being bitten by a dog, jackal, horse, camel, boar or man. [[Yaj.|Yāj.]] III. 305 prescribes the performance of a hundred prāṇāyāmas for the destruction of all sins, of [[upapatakas|upapātakas]] (lesser [[sing|sins]] described in Yāj. III. 234–242) and of sins for which no specific prāyaścitta is provided. Manu II. 83 ( = Vasiṣṭha X. 5 and Viṣṇudharmasūtra 55.83) states 'the one syllable (om) is (the representative of) the highest brahma and prāṇāyāmas are the highest tapas'.

It may interest readers to note that the great Jaina monk, ācārya [[Homacandra|Hemacandra]], condemns prāṇāyāmas by saying that they allow no rest to the mind, that there is physical effort in pūraka, kumbhaka and recaka and that prānāyāma is an obstacle to mukti.[^2368]

In kumbhaka performed after pūraka the nerves, heart and lungs are under strain and permanent damage to these may result from careless or [[basty|hasty]] practice of kumbhaka after pūraka. Those suffering from lung or cardiac affections should not enter upon the practice of prāṇāyāma on their own initiative but must first consult an expert. Swami Vivekananda said long ago that all students of Yoga[^2369] 'are expressly and earnestly reminded

[^2366]: [[ma: mited e TUTE I ETROTTE T trgar \#\#:1 9. D.|ततः क्षीयते प्रकाशावरणम् । धारणासु च योग्यता मनसः । यो. सू.]] II. 52-53 ; '[[प्राणायामानभ्यश्यतोऽस्य|प्राणायामानभ्यस्यतोऽस्य]] योगिनः क्षीयते विवेकज्ञानावरणीयं कर्म। ...[[तथा चोचम् । तपोन|तथा चोक्तम् । तपो न]] परं प्राणायामात्ततो विशुद्धिर्मलानां दीप्तिश्च ज्ञानस्य । इति। भाष्य.

Figures 27 to 40 (pp. 68-89 in Shri Kuvalayānanda's popular handbook on Prāṇāyāma, part one) illustrate the three (pūraka &c.) in prāṇāyāma of the best scientific type.]]

[[P1442]]that with few exceptions Yoga can safely be learnt by direct contact with a teacher'. The fact, that the Yogasūtra [[oon. taining|containing]] 195 sūtras in all devotes only five sūtras [[(II. 49-53 )|(II. 49-53)]] to prāṇāyāma and that even these five are of a very general [[Dature|nature]], indicates that [[Patarijali|Patañjali]] intended that the [[yogio|yogin]] should not practise prāṇāyāma by merely listening to or reading [[those|these]] sūtras but should approach an expert yogin for instruction and guidance.

It should be noticed that Patañjali does not require in his definition of prāṇāyāma the mental or inaudible repetition of any syllable like 'om' or any mantra like the [[Gayatrı.|Gāyatrī.]] But the Smṛtis prescribe the practice of [[prāṇāyame|prāṇāyāma]] every day in the [[Sandhyāvandada.|Sandhyāvandana.]] Yāj. (I. 22) [[proscribes|prescribes]][^2370] that a man of the three higher varṇas must every day take a bath, perform mārjana (sprinkling water with kuśas) with the mantras ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] X. 9. 1-3 '[[apo hi ṣthā|āpo hi ṣṭhā]] &c.'), practise prāṇāyāma, worship of the sun and japa of [[Gayatri|Gāyatrī]] ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] III. 62. 10) and that prāṇāyāma comprises the japa three times of [[Gayatri|Gāyatrī]] preceded by the [[Vyābstis|Vyāhṛtis]], each being preceded by Om and followed by the [[firas.|Śiras.]] Prāṇāyāma as described by Yāj. is prescribed even in modern times for the morning and evening [[Sandbyā|Sandhyā]] adorations. [[Prānā yama|Prāṇāyāma]] with mental repetition of 'Om' or [[a.mantra'|a 'mantra']] is [[oalled|called]] 'sagarbha'[^2371] or [['sabija,'|'sabīja,']] while prāṇāyāma not so [[acoompanied|accompanied]]

[^2370]: [[स्नानमब्दैवतैमन्त्रैर्मार्जनं|स्नानमब्दैवतैर्मन्त्रैर्मार्जनं]] प्राणसंयमः। सूर्यस्य चाप्युपस्थानं गायत्र्याः प्रत्यहं [[जपः।|जपः ॥]] [[गायत्री|गायत्रीं]] शिरसा [[साध|सार्धं]] जपेद् [[ध्याहृतिपूधिकाम्|व्याहृतिपूर्विकाम्]] । प्रतिप्रणवसंयुक्तां [[त्रिरय|त्रिरयं]] [[प्राणसंयमः।|प्राणसंयमः ॥]] याज्ञ. I. 22–23. The second verse occurs in [[fag|वसिष्ठ]] 25. 13, [[arrat.|बृहद्योगिया.]] VIII. 3, [[HfHita|अग्निसंहिता]] verse 298. The whole japa for [[TIMITETA|प्राणायाम]] would be [[sī y ty: 31 m: 11 AR:|ओं भूः ओं भुवः ओं स्वः ओं महः]] [[ओंजनः|ओं जनः]] ओं तपः ओं सत्यं ओं तत्सवितुर्वरेण्यं भर्गो देवस्य धीमहि । धियो यो नः [[प्रचोदयात।|प्रचोदयात् ।]] [[Onu seat S a: Fri. Fl.|ओं आपो ज्योती रसोऽमृतं ब्रह्मभूर्भुवः स्वरोम् ।]] This has to be repeated thrice in the सन्ध्यावन्दन. The words आपो ज्यो ... रोम् are called [[शिर:|शिरः]] of गायत्री. [[Videgr.|Vide बृहद्योगिया.]] VIII. 4-6 q. by स्मृतिच. I p. 141 as योगया.

II. 52 and by anerai p. 1022 ( which explaios FP11: FAFI:, on fist Tat:). [[P1443]]is called 'agarbha' or [['abija'|'abīja']] and it is said that [["sagarbha'|'sagarbha']] is the better of the two. Śāntiparva ([[304. 9. or. ed.=Ch. ed. 316.9-10|304. 9. cr. ed. = Ch. ed. 316. 9-10]]) speaks of saguṇa and [[nirgupa|nirguṇa]] prāṇāyāma. The [[Yogabhāsy &|Yogabhāṣya]] (on [[Y. 8. II, 52|Y. S. II. 52]]) quotes a passage[^2372] 'there is no higher tapas than prāṇāyāma, therefrom arises purification of the taints and knowledge flashes forth.'

The H. Y. P. speaks of eight kinds of Prāṇāyāma (in II. 44). The two most important ones, viz [[Ujjāyi|Ujjāyī]] and Bhastrikā are described by Śrī Kuvalayānanda in chap. IV. pp. 67-68 and chap. VI. pp. 101-115 respectively in his handbook on [[Prāṇā yāma,|Prāṇāyāma,]] part one and the other six viz. Sūryabhedana, [[Sitkāri,|Sītkārī,]] [[Sitali,|Śītalī,]] [[Bhramari,|Bhrāmarī,]] [[Mūrochā,|Mūrcchā,]] [[Plāvini|Plāvinī]] in pp. 6–25 of the handbook part 2. H. Y. P. (II. 48-70) describes all the eight at some length.

Dr. [[Role|Rele]] in his work 'Mysterious [[Kundalini'|Kuṇḍalinī']] provides at the very beginning a diagram of the autonomic nervous system according to the Western anatomical science, showing the six [[oakres|cakras]] and identifying the positions of those six and the [[sahasraracakra|sahasrāracakra]] (lit. thousand-petalled i. e. having [[pumberless|numberless]] petals). He propounded an original view that the [[Kundalini|Kuṇḍalinī]] is the right vagus nerve. His book is very interesting and he has explored a very large field of yogic practices. He has very thoroughly utilized his deep knowledge of Western anatomy and physiology, but he himself admits in his preface (p. 2) that the explanations of the various [[Yogio|Yogic]] practices and phenomena given by him are possible suggestions only. But it may be noted that Sir John Woodroffe, who made a deep study of Yoga and of Tantra works and who wrote a foreword to Dr. [[Role's|Rele's]] work, was not prepared to accept Dr. [[Role's|Rele's]] identification of [[Kundalini|Kuṇḍalinī]] with the right Vagus Nerve (p. ii) and holds that [[Kundalint|Kuṇḍalinī]] is not a nerve or any other physical substance or mental faculty but rather the Ground Substance of both ([[Forge word|Foreword]] p. iii). Similarly, Shri [[Kuvalayanand|Kuvalayānanda]] (in his popular handbook on [[prāpāyāma|prāṇāyāma]] part 1, p. 57 n. 3), after referring to Dr. Rele's admission about his explanations being possible [[sugges tions|suggestions]] only, points out that Dr. Rele had not tried a single [[experi ment|experiment]] in the laboratory nor had he taken much [[oare|care]] to consult the practical experience of the students of Yoga and he states that to him the whole book of Dr. [[Role|Rele]] seems to be of doubtful scientific value. He goes further and [[apports|asserts]] that Swami

[^2372]: [[Eur ar api MTAJATTAT Fagfaserati Mata IBRANI mava on I1, 52; compare fagnat e III, 280, 4 g ar ni विजातीनां सथा सपा|तथा चोक्तम् । तपो न परं प्राणायामात्ततो विशुद्धिर्मलानां दीप्तिश्च ज्ञानस्य । इति । योगभाष्य on II. 52; compare विष्णुधर्मोत्तर III. 280. 4 प्राणायामात्परं नास्ति द्विजातीनां तथा तपः ।]]

[[P1444]]Vivekananda's lectures on [[Rajayoga|Rājayoga]] suffer from the [[game|same]] [[draw. backs|drawbacks]] as the work of Dr. [[Relo|Rele]] does. Śrī [[Kuvalayanand|Kuvalayānanda]] shows (on pp. 121-126) the value of [[pripāyāma|prāṇāyāma]] for physical health and the proper functioning of the lungs, the organs of digestion, the liver, pancreas and kidneys and asserts that the spiritual value of Prāṇāyāma is very great.

[[Pratyahāra|Pratyāhāra]] (withdrawal of the [[sonse orgads|sense organs]] from the objects of [[senso )|sense)]] is defined[^2373] in Y. S. II. 54 as 'when the senses have no contact with (are withdrawn from) their [[objecte|objects]] (because the mind is restrained) and thereby resemble the state of the mind itself, there is pratyāhāra.' When the mind, being restrained by the yogin, does not come in contact with the objects of [[senge,|sense,]] viz. sound, touch, form, taste, smell, the organs of [[songe|sense]] also become disconnected with them, the organs come to resemble the mind itself (hence the words 'anukāra iva' in the sūtra). From this (non-contact) results the complete mastery over the senses. The idea is that in restraining the mind from the objects of sense the organs also are withdrawn from contact with them. Since the mind is made ekāgra (one-pointed) the sense organs together with the mind do not cognise or apprehend the objects. Pratyāhāra is the checking of the outgoing activities of the mind and freeing it from being the slave of the senses. The [[Sāntiparva|Śāntiparva]] (cr. ed. 188. 5-7 = Ch. ed. 195. 6-7) speaks of it. The [[Vigpupurāṇa|Viṣṇupurāṇa]] in V. 10. 14 (in describing [[Sarad,|Śarad,]] i.e. autumn, states '[[Sarad|Śarad]] removed the turbidness of waters as pratyāhāra withdraws the organs from their objects') refers to pratyāhāra[^2374]

[^2373]: [[स्वविषयासंपयोगे चित्रस्वरूपानुकार|स्वविषयासंप्रयोगे चित्तस्वरूपानुकार]] इवेन्द्रियाणां प्रत्याहारः। ततः परमा [[पश्यते Presyong i 1. 8.|वश्यतेन्द्रियाणाम् । यो. सू.]] II. 54-55. The word [[wareTT|प्रत्याहार]] is formed from the root [[with sia and 31.|हृ with प्रति and आ.]] The [[TSINUE|राजमार्तण्ड]] explains '[[agir faggru; ariqariga TATRA HTYIETT:|इन्द्रियाणि विषयेभ्यः प्रतीपमानीयन्तेऽस्मिन्निति प्रत्याहारः'.]] The word [[eit|प्रत्याहार]] literally means 'bringing back'. The [[joy|भाष्य]] explains '[['स्त्रविषयसंप्रयोगाभावे चित्तस्वरूपानुकार इति चित्तनिरोधे चित्तवलिरुद्धानीन्द्रियाणि नेतन्द्रि यजयबदुपायान्तरमपेक्षन्ते । यथा मधुकरराज मक्षिका उत्पतन्तमनुत्पतन्ति निविशन्तमनु निविशन्ते autem faara Paegriran PERTUTT:1).|स्वविषयसंप्रयोगाभावे चित्तस्वरूपानुकार इति चित्तनिरोधे चित्तवन्निरुद्धानीन्द्रियाणि नेतरेन्द्रियजयवदुपायान्तरमपेक्षन्ते । यथा मधुकरराजं मक्षिका उत्पतन्तमनूत्पतन्ति निविशन्तमनु निविशन्ते तथेन्द्रियाणि चित्तनिरोधे निरुद्धानीत्येष प्रत्याहारः ।'.]] The illustration of the queen bee and the honey-making bees occurs in [[Prasoa|Praśna]] Up. II. 4 [['agu RTNET मधुकरराजानमुत्क्रामन्तं सर्वा एवोकामन्ते तस्मिश्च प्रतिष्ठमाने सर्वा एव प्रातिष्ठन्ते। एवं वायन|'तद्यथा मक्षिका मधुकरराजानमुत्क्रामन्तं सर्वा एवोत्क्रामन्ते तस्मिश्च प्रतिष्ठमाने सर्वा एव प्रातिष्ठन्ते । एवं वाङ्मनश्चक्षुः श्रोत्रं च ।'.]] This sūtra is variously interpreted but the [[bhāsya|bhāṣya]] follows the view of [[Jaigigavya.|Jaigīṣavya.]]

[^2374]: शब्दादिष्वनुषक्तानि निगृह्याक्षाणि [[पोगवित् । कुशिताकारीणि|योगवित् । कुर्याच्चित्तानुकारीणि]] [[प्रत्याहार परायणः॥|प्रत्याहारपरायणः ॥]] वश्यता परमा तेन जायते निश्चलात्मनाम् । [[इन्द्रियाणामवश्येसैने|इन्द्रियाणामवश्यैस्तैर्न]] योगी योगसाधकः। [[fecuny.|विष्णुपु.]] VI. 7. 43-44. These are also quoted by [[998 (AMATOR P. 173) aod by sarah|कृत्यकल्प० (मोक्षकाण्ड p. 173) and by अपरार्क]] p. 1025. [[Tho are .|The मार्क० पु.]] (39. 41 of Cal. ed. = 36. 41-42 of [[Veok,|Venk.]] edition) says '[[gagamistasuna emot vaizaTw:BEOnagrama SeriETT taa: Tga:|शब्दादिभ्यो निवृत्तानि यदक्षाणि यतात्मभिः । प्रत्याह्रियन्ते योगेन प्रत्याहारस्ततः स्मृतः ॥]] q. by [[ft 90 (Ano po|कृत्यकल्प० (मोक्ष p.]] 173).

[[P1445]]as '[[Indriyanindriyarthebhyaḥ pratyahāra ivāharat'.| Vacaspati|Indriyāṇīndriyārthebhyaḥ pratyāhāra ivāharat'. Vācaspati]] quotes two verses from the Viṣṇupurāṇa which employ the very characteristic words of the Y. S. and probably borrow the idea and language from the Y. S. The Dharmasūtra of [[Dovala|Devala]][^2375] explains [[pratyahāra|प्रत्याहार]] as '[[establisbing|establishing]] (or yoking) the mind to the soul by bringing it back (to the soul) when it has swerved from the path of Yoga, because of its subtlety, its restlessness, its thoughtlessness or its force'. The Kūrmapurāṇa[^2376] defines it as 'the suppression or mastery over the organs of sense that are naturally attracted to objects of sense'. Vide [[Sāntiparva|Śāntiparva]] ([[07. ed. 232. 13 )|cr. ed. 232. 13]]) also.

It appears that when the Chān. Up. (in VIII. 15) [[opumeratos|enumerates]] the circumstances which enable a person to reach the worlds of [[brahmu|brahman]] it has an idea of [[pratyābāra|pratyāhāra]] in the word '[[atmani sarvendriyāṇi sampratisthāpya'|ātmani sarvendriyāṇi sampratiṣṭhāpya']] ([[(* when|'when]] he concentrates all his senses in the self').

The third pāda of the Y. S. is called '[[Vibhati-pāda'|Vibhūti-pāda']] (pāda that deals with the [[super-pormal|super-normal]] powers of the yogin). The word 'vibhūti' occurs in [[Praspa 5, 4|Praśna 5. 4]] where it is stated that he who contemplates on Om of the dvimātra type is taken to the world of the moon where he enjoys [['vibhāti'|'vibhūti']] and again returns to the earth. There the word probably means nothing more than prosperous life. The third pāda first deals with the last three of the [[sight angas|eight aṅgas]] of Yoga, the first five being called [[bahiranga|bahiraṅga]] (indirect helpers of [[samprajñātajamādbi),|samprajñāta-samādhi),]] while the last [[thras|three]] are said to be [[antaranga|antaraṅga]] of [[samprajñāta-samadhi|samprajñāta-samādhi]] (but even these three are [[bahiranga|bahiraṅga]] with reference to [[pirblia|nirbīja]] Yoga, [[og be latter|as the latter]] may occur [[oven|even]] in the absence of the three 'dhāraṇā' &c. These three are called [[dharapā, dhyāna and samadhi|dhāraṇā, dhyāna and samādhi]] and these three when practised on the [[game|same]] object are called [[sarnyama|saṁyama]], which is a technical term in the Yoga system. Vibhūtis are the results of [[samyama|saṁyama]] of various kinds. Instead of employing the three words [[dhāraṇa|dhāraṇā]], dhyāna and samādhi Patañjali [[explogs|employs]] expressly or [[implied,|impliedly]] the word [['gamyama'|'saṁyama']] in most of the [[sāttas|sūtras]] from 16 to 52 of the 3rd pāda.

[^2375]: [[अशुसाशापल्याडवाइलबजादा पोमनटस्य मनसा पुनः पलानीपाव RETETT120 4. by Father (Argp. 173 ); m0 p. 1025 maibes that it to entic and reads HABITATU Trane RH (ww akorld read T41** ), ms (Apto p. 173 ) explaios' HORAS|अशुभात्प्रत्याहृत्याकुशलान्मनसो योगभ्रष्टस्य मनसः पुनः प्रत्यानीयात्मन्येव स्थापनं प्रत्याहारः । देवल q. by कृत्यकल्प० (मोक्ष० p. 173); अपरार्क p. 1025 ascribes this sūtra to हारीत and reads 'अशुभात्प्रत्याहृत्याकुशलान्मनसा योगभ्रष्टस्य ... प्रत्याहारः' (we should read ०हृत्या योग०). कृत्यकल्प० (मोक्ष० p. 173) explains 'अकुशलान्मनसा'.]]

[^2376]: [[pag grant Permat fag FTHA: Para sitt af m a \# 11. 11. 38. , from 41, 101 Prat We Are पाया। वस्याहरल वरपा प्रयासउपदे । पुरषा mean'विवेमवनिया,|इन्द्रियाणां चरतां विषयेषु स्वभावतः । निग्रहः प्रोच्यते सद्भिः प्रत्याहारस्तु सत्तम ॥ कूर्म II. 11. 38. स्कन्द, काशीखण्ड 41. 101 'इन्द्रियाणां हि चरतां विषयेषु यदृच्छया । यत्प्रत्याहरणं युक्त्या प्रत्याहारः स उच्यते ॥', युक्त्या means 'नियमोपपादनेन'.]]

[[P1446]]Dhāraṇā, dhyāna and [[samadhi|samādhi]] are said to be the direct auxiliaries of Yoga and are three successive stages, the preceding one leading to the next. [[Dhārapā|Dhāraṇā]] is the holding of the mind to a certain spot or point[^2377] or object. The bhāṣya explains that the mind is to be held down to such spots (in one's body) as the navel, the heart-lotus, the head, the light (in the eye), the tip of the nose, the tip of the tongue and the like or on external objects (such as images of gods in various forms). In this stage attention has to be firmly held upon the [[choson|chosen]] object for a period of [[timo|time]] determined by the will of the yoga aspirant. In this stage there are three elements, the subject, the [[objoot|object]] and the act of concentration. The next stage is dhyāna, which will be described immediately below. The Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa speaks of ten dhāraṇās on the different parts of the yogin's own body[^2378] and is in accordance with the plural 'dhāraṇāsu ca yogyatā manasaḥ' (in Y. S. II. 53) employed by [[Patanjali|Patañjali]] himself. The [[Asvamedhikaparva|Āśvamedhikaparva]] also employs the plural 'dhāraṇāsu'. Vide the [[Santiparva|Śāntiparva]] ([[er. ed. 188.8-12 %D Ch. ed. 195.8|cr. ed. 188. 8-12 = Ch. ed. 195. 8]]). The [[Yajnavalkyasmrti|Yājñavalkyasmṛti]][^2379] in a succint manner brings

[^2377]: देशबन्धश्चित्तस्य धारणा । तत्र [[प्रत्ययकतानता|प्रत्ययैकतानता]] ध्यानम् । यो. सू. III. 1, 2; the [[भाग्य|भाष्य]] on these is: [[नाभिचके हृदय पुण्डरीके मूस्नि|नाभिचक्रे हृदयपुण्डरीके मूर्ध्नि]] ज्योतिषि नासिकाये [[जिव्हान|जिह्वाग्र]] इत्येवमादित देशेषु [[चाहो|बाह्ये]] वा विषये चित्तस्य वृत्तिमात्रेण बन्ध इति धारणा । [[तस्मिग्देशे ध्येयालम्बमस्य|तस्मिन्देशे ध्येयालम्बनस्य]] प्रत्ययस्यैकतानता सदृशः प्रवाहः प्रत्ययान्तरेणापरामृष्टो ध्यानम् ।. The [[लिपु|लिङ्गपु०]] I. 8. 42-43 appears to echo the words of यो. सू. in 'चित्तस्य धारणा [[पोक्ता|प्रोक्ता]] स्थानबन्धः समासतः । ...तत्रैकचित्तता ध्यानं प्रत्ययान्तरवर्जितम् ।'. The Upaniṣads speak of the heart as lotus (vide above note 1717 and [[शराचार्य|शङ्कराचार्य]] on वे. [[स.|सू.]] I. [[3, 14-21 दहर उसरेग्य:|3. 14-21 दहर उत्तरेभ्यः]] &c.). ज्योतिषि probably refers to the Puruṣa in the eye (as in छा. VIII. [[7..|7. 4]] or IV. 15. 1 (य एषोऽक्षिणि पुरुषो दृश्यत एष आत्मेति होवाच) or the Lord in one's heart; वाचस्पति explains [[बाहो|बाह्ये]] वा विषये by quoting several verses of the [[विष्ण. पुराण|विष्णुपुराण]] VI. 7. 77-85 where it speaks of contemplation on the form of [[Visma|Viṣṇu]] with a benign face, lotus-like eyes, wearing [[earrings|ear-rings]] and having the [[Srivatsa|Śrīvatsa]] jewel on [[bis cbest,|his chest,]] with four or eight long arms, wearing a yellow garment and holding conch, [[sāriga|śārṅga]] bow, mace,

[^2378]: [[पाक्नाभ्यां हवये|प्राङ्नाभ्यां हृदये]] चाथ तृतीये च तयोरसि । कण्ठे मुखे नासिकाग्रे [[नेत्रभूमाय. मर्थ|नेत्रभ्रूमध्ये मूर्धसु ॥]] किंचित्तस्मात्परस्मिंश्च धारणा परमा स्मृता । दशैता धारणाः प्राप्य [[प्रामोत्यक्षरसालयताम् ।।|प्राप्नोत्यक्षरसाम्यताम् ॥]] मार्कण्डेय (36. [[44-45 =39.|44-45 = 39.]] 44-45, Cal. ed.), [[गरुडपु (|गरुडपु० (I.]] 226. 21-22) q. by अपरार्क p. 1023 and [[फत्यकल्प (मोक्ष|कृत्यकल्प० (मोक्ष०]] p. 173). Compare कूर्म '[[हरपुण्डरीके माग्यो|हृत्पुण्डरीके नाभ्यां]] वा [[मग्नि पर्वस|मूर्ध्नि पर्वतमस्तके]] । [[एवमाविषु देशेष|एवमादिषु देशेषु]] धारणा चित्तबन्धनम् ॥' II. 11. 39; तथैव [[तपते|युक्तो]] योगी धारणासु समाहितः । [[प्रामोरयाणु|प्राप्नोत्याशु]] परं स्थानं [[लषमुक हवागः॥|लक्ष्यमुक्त इवाशुगः ॥]] आश्वमेधिक 19. 37.

[^2379]: [[ऊरुस्योत्तानच्चरणः सम्ये न्यस्योसरं|ऊरुस्थोत्तानचरणः सव्ये न्यस्योत्तरं]] करम्। उत्तानं [[किंचिगुलाम्य मुख विडम्य|किंचिदुन्नम्य मुखं विष्टभ्य]] चोरसा । निमीलिताक्षः [[सत्वस्थो|सत्त्वस्थो]] दन्तैर्दन्तानसंस्पृशन् । [[तालस्थापलजिहच संपतास्या|तालुस्थाचलजिह्वश्च संवृतास्यः]] सुनिश्चलः। [[संनिरुध्येग्दियग्राम|संनिरुध्येन्द्रियग्रामं]] [[नातिनीचोशिसासनः|नातिनीचोच्छ्रितासनः]] । द्विगुणं [[विराण|त्रिगुणं]] वापि [[प्राणायाम मुपक्रमेत्|प्राणायाममुपक्रमेत्]] । ततो ध्येया स्थितो [[योऽसो हदये|योऽसौ हृदये]] दीपवत्प्रभुः। धारयेचत्रचारमानं धारणां [[धारयम्|धारयन्]] बुधः ॥ याज्ञ. III. 198-201. These except the last are quoted by कृत्यकल्प० (मोक्षकाण्ड p. 180) and occur in स्कन्द, काशीखण्ड 41. 69–71 and the last (verse 201) is q. by कृत्यकल्प० (मोक्ष on p. 194). Vide figure 16 in Shri Kuvalayānanda's handbook on 'Āsanas' (pp. 54-55) for पद्मासन. These verses in या. are commented upon by Viśvarūpa, the Mitākṣarā and Aparārka. Therefore they cannot be said to be later interpolations.

[[P1447]]in the [[argas|aṅgas]] from [[ābana|āsana]] to [[dhāraṇā and dhyana|dhāraṇā and dhyāna]] as follows: (The yogin) [[oocupying & sest|occupying a seat]] neither too high nor too low, having placed his upturned heels on his thighs and having placed on the left (palm [[placad|placed]] on the upturned right heel) his other (right) palm (turned upwards), having slightly [[Taised|raised]] his face and having braced up his body with his (raised) chest, having closed the eyes, being free from rajas and tamas, not allowing the upper and lower rows of teeth to touch, holding his tongue firmly in the palate, and allowing no shaking of the body, covering the mouth, holding back the organs of sense from their objects, should perform prāṇāyāma of the twofold or threefold type (of 24 or 36 mātrās), should [[contemplete|contemplate]] on the Lord that occupies his heart like a lamp, and fix his mind on that Lord by way of Dhāraṇā. [[Dovala|Devala]] states[^2380] that [[dhārapa|dhāraṇā]] is the holding in check of the body, senses, mind, intellect and ātman (egoism).

M. Charles Baudouin in 'Suggestion and auto-suggestion' [[to, by Elder and Cedar Paul (od. of 1922, Allep and Unwin ) says (on p. 150). As|tr. by Eden and Cedar Paul (ed. of 1922, Allen and Unwin) says (on p. 150) 'As]] one of the curiosities of history and further as a lesson in humility we must point out that the states just described under the names of collectedness, contention and [[auto hypnosis|auto-hypnosis]] are described with considerable psychological acumen (though not in modern psychological terminology) in the precepts by which for centuries past the Yogis of Hindustan have been accustomed to attain to self-mastery' and the author refers to Pratyāhāra and Dhāraṇā in this connection and on p. 151 remarks that auto-hypnosis is encountered in Yoga but it is tinged with mysticism because the sacred word 'om' is repeated hundreds of times.

Dhyāna (contemplation) is the one-pointedness (continuous flow) of the apprehension of the object contemplated upon,

[^2380]: [[Egurrat STEVIE TROTTI, E q. by secret p. 1025, B ee (ATH p. 174). Bref says 'ERHTATE:, the gove4o Bays nothing on MIGHT|शरीरेन्द्रियमनोबुद्ध्यात्मनां धारणाद्धारणा । देवल q. by अपरार्क p. 1025, कृत्यकल्प० (मोक्ष p. 174). अपरार्क says 'अत्रात्माहंकारः'; the कृत्यकल्प० says nothing on आत्मा.]]

[[P1448]]unaffected by any other idea or apprehension. The Upaniṣads[^2381] [[insiet|insist]] upon [[dhyana,|dhyāna,]] e. g. [[Mundaka|Muṇḍaka]] says 'contemplate upon the Self in the form of Om'; the [[Br. Up.|Bṛ. Up.]] has the famous passage 'the self should be seen, heard, understood and reflected upon.' [[Tho Chan. Up. (VIL6.2)|The Chān. Up. (VII. 6. 2)]] employs the word 'dhyāna' in the sense of 'concentrating all thoughts on one object'. The [[Sv. Up. (I.3)|Śv. Up. (I. 3)]] mentions dhyānayoga and also [[Gita 18. 52|Gītā 18. 52]] does so ([[dhyanayoga. paro|dhyānayogaparo]] nityam). The [[Santiparva|Śāntiparva]] (cr. ed. 188. 13 ff = Ch. ed. 195. 13-18) speaks of dhyāna. The [[Kr̥tyakalpataru (on Moksa|Kṛtyakalpataru (on Mokṣa]] p. 181) quotes a long prose passage from [[Devala-dharmasutra|Devala-dharmasūtra]] about dhyāna. The [[Vispupurāṇa|Viṣṇupurāṇa]][^2382] closely agreeing with the [[Yogabhāsya|Yogabhāṣya]] in phraseology describes dhyāna as the one-pointed [[guccession|succession]] of the apprehension of the Lord's form, unmixed with another idea, brought about by the practice of the first six [[angas|aṅgas]] of [[Yoga/Apararka|Yoga. Aparārka]] (pp. 1025-27) quotes a long prose passage from [[Vishudharmasutra ( chap.97),|Viṣṇudharmasūtra (chap. 97),]] which asks the yogin to contemplate upon the all-pervading and all-powerful Lord who is free from [[gupas|guṇas]] (sattva, rajas, tamas), is beyond the twenty-four [[tattyas|tattvas]] and who is imperceptible to the senses, and that, if he be unable to focuss his mind on formless God all at once, to

[^2381]: आत्मा वा अरे द्रष्टव्यः श्रोतव्यो [[मन्तग्यो|मन्तव्यो]] निदिध्यासितव्यः। बृह. उप. II. 4. 5; ओमित्येवं ध्यायथ आत्मानम् । मुण्डक [[II. 2.6.|II. 2. 6.]] निदिध्यासितव्यः is the potential passive participle of the desiderative form of [[ध्ये with नि.|ध्यै with नि.]] The छा. उप. VII. 6 is '[[ज्यान वाप चित्ताद्धयः।|ध्यानं वाव चित्ताद्भूयः ।]] [[पृथिवी... घ्यायन्तीव|पृथिवी ... ध्यायन्तीव]] [[देवमनुण्याः।|देवमनुष्याः ।]] [[तस्माप इद मनुष्याणां महा प्रामवन्ति ध्यानापादांशा इव|तस्माद्य इह मनुष्याणां महत्तां प्राप्नुवन्ति ध्यानापादांशा इवैव]] ते भवन्ति। ... ध्यानमुपास्स्वेति। The earth is motionless, just as a yogin when deep in contemplation is motionless and therefore it is said "the earth is as it were engaged in contemplation."

[^2382]: [[वदपप्रत्ययकायसन्ततिश्चान्यनिःस्पृहा|तद्रूपप्रत्ययैकाग्रसन्ततिश्चान्यनिःस्पृहा]] । [[तद ध्यानं प्रथमैरङ्गैः षभिनिव्यायते सुप।|तद् ध्यानं प्रथमैरङ्गैः षड्भिनिष्पाद्यते नृप ॥]] विष्णुपु. VI. 7. 91 q. by वाचस्पति, कृत्यकल्प० (मोक्षकाण्ड p. 175) and अपरार्क p. 1026 who explains '[[ब्रह्मरूपप्रत्ययास्मिका सन्तति: प्रवाहः सा विषयान्तरासंस्मटा सती ध्यानमित्युच्यते।|ब्रह्मरूपप्रत्ययात्मिका सन्ततिः प्रवाहः सा विषयान्तरासंस्पृष्टा सती ध्यानमित्युच्यते ।' ;]] सगुणं वर्णभेदेन निर्गुणं केवलं मतम् । समन्त्रं सगुणं विद्धि निर्गुणं मन्त्रवर्जितम् ॥ स्कन्द, काशी० 41. 120. One verse from [[दृसिंहपुराण|नृसिंहपुराण]] is repeated even now in worship viz. ध्येयः सदा सवितृमण्डलमध्यवर्ती नारायणः [[सरसिजासन संनिविष्टः ।|सरसिजासनसंनिविष्टः ।]] [[केयूरवान मकरकुण्डलवान्|केयूरवान् मकरकुण्डलवान्]] किरीटी हारी [[हिरण्मयवपुर्धतशाचकः|हिरण्मयवपुर्धृतशङ्खचक्रः]] ॥ 26. 17 q. by कृत्यकल्प० (मोक्ष p. 192); [[हस्थस्य|हृत्स्थस्य]] ध्यानयोगेन देवदेवस्य दर्शनम्। ध्यानं [[मोक्त|प्रोक्तं]] प्रवक्ष्यामि ध्यानयोगमतः शुभम् । शङ्खस्मृति VII. 16. This is followed by seven verses, two of which are अणोरणीयान् and [[दाहमेतं|वेदाहमेतं]] (VII. 20 and 23) that are the same as [[श्वेताश्च.|श्वेताश्व.]] उप. III. 20 and 8 respectively, and all the eight verses are q. by [[कृस्यकल्प|कृत्यकल्प०]] (मोक्ष p. 193). The editor did not identify them from [[शमा|शङ्ख;]] [[पहिर्मुखानि सर्वाणि कुत्ता चान्तर्मुखानि ।|बहिर्मुखानि सर्वाणि कृत्वा चान्तर्मुखानि वै ।]] [[मनसेवेन्द्रियग्राम|मनसैवेन्द्रियग्रामं]] मनश्चात्मनि योजयेत् । सर्वभावविनिर्मुक्तं क्षेत्रज्ञं ब्रह्मणि न्यसेत् । एतज्ज्ञानं च ध्यानं च [[शेषोन्यो अन्धविस्तर|शेषोऽन्यो ग्रन्थविस्तरः ॥]] दक्षस्मृति VII. 20-21. These and the following [[ive|five]] verses are q. by [[करयकल्प.|कृत्यकल्प०]] (मोक्ष pp. 207-208) which the editor did not identify. The verse सर्वभाव ... [[विस्तर|विस्तरः]] is q. by अपरार्क p. 970 (reads [[एतद् ध्यानं च योगच|एतद् ध्यानं च योगश्च]]).

[[P1449]]gradually rise up from [[Pșthyl|Pṛthvī]] and the other elements, mind, intellect, the soul, avyakta ([[upmanifested|unmanifested]] Spirit); if he be unable to do even this he should contemplate on the person that is like a lamp in his own heart (lotus); if this is impossible, he should contemplate upon the form of [[Vasudeva|Vāsudeva]] having on his chest vanamālā, having four arms, holding the conch, the quoit, mace and lotus; and adds that he secures whatever he contemplates upon and that this is the secret of dhyāna. This shows that dhyāna is either [[saguna or nirguna|saguṇa or nirguṇa]] as stated by the [[Padmapurana IV.84, 80-86|Padmapurāṇa IV. 84. 80-86]] [[(pirguna)|(nirguṇa)]] and IV. 84. 88-96 (saguṇa) or [[sūkāra and nirākūra|sākāra and nirākāra]] (Padmapurāṇa, II. 80. 70, 77-78). The [[Vispoparāṇa|Viṣṇupurāṇa]] (VI. 7. 78-90) contains a passage similar to the [[Visnu|Viṣṇu]] Dh. S. Skanda ([[Kāśikhanda,|Kāśīkhaṇḍa,]] chap. 41. 19) speaks of dhyāna as [[saguna and nirguna|saguṇa and nirguṇa]] and defines [[saguna|saguṇa]] as depending on different forms or as accompanied by repetition of mantras and [[nirgupa|nirguṇa]] as 'not depending on any particular form or as not accompanied by mantras'. The [[Narasimhapurāṇa|Narasiṁhapurāṇa]] (17. 11-28 and 26. 17) describes at some length the form in which Nārāyaṇa or Viṣṇu is to be contemplated upon and these verses are quoted by the [[Kr̥tya kalpataru|Kṛtyakalpataru]] (Mokṣa, pp. 191-192). The [[Saṅkha-smrti|Śaṅkha-smṛti]] states that one has realization ([[darsana|darśana]]) of the Lord of gods that abides in the heart (of every person) by means of dhyānayoga and describes the mode of dhyāna. [[Dakṣasmrti|Dakṣasmṛti]] also emphasizes dhyāna and jñāna (correct knowledge) and states that [[descrip tion|description]] of others serves no purpose beyond adding to the bulk of the [[workIn this stage|work. In this stage]] there is only the duality of subject and object, there is no awareness of effort to tie down the mind on an object (as in mere dhāraṇā).

[[Samadhi|Samādhi]] (concentration and absorption)—Dhyāna itself is [[Samadhi,|Samādhi,]] when only the contemplated object shines forth and when dhyāna is as if emptied of its own form because there is no apprehension of dhyāna as distinct from dhyeya.[^2383] When

[^2383]: [[देवार्थमात्र निर्मास स्वरूपचन्यमिव समाधिः । अयमेकत्र संयमः। तदपि पहिर Patre i 1. a. III. 3, 4, 8. The eye on these three is: en 4419 निर्भासं प्रत्ययात्मकेन स्वरूपेण शून्यमिव यंदा भवति ध्येयस्वभावावेशात् तदा समाधिरित्युच्यते । सदेतधारणा-ध्यान समाधि-त्रयमेकत्र संयमः। एकविश्वाणि त्रीणि साधनानि संयम इत्युच्यते। तदस्व अयस्य तान्त्रिकी परिभाषा संयम इति । तवपन्तर साधनत्रयं निजस्व योगस्व NIE I hai aur UFC 1. The trwarde explains the word FATT as a nd its rara nenu w a na: 1. The paper p. 118 (Kashi series, 1930) brings out the distinction between hasira and 2 a FATH as follows: UTCAI THAT A F FA.|तदेवार्थमात्रनिर्भासं स्वरूपशून्यमिव समाधिः । अयमेकत्र संयमः । तदपि बहिरङ्गं निर्बीजस्य । यो. सू. III. 3, 4, 8. The भाष्य on these three is: ध्यानमेव ध्येयाकारनिर्भासं प्रत्ययात्मकेन स्वरूपेण शून्यमिव यदा भवति ध्येयस्वभावावेशात् तदा समाधिरित्युच्यते । तदेतद्धारणा-ध्यान-समाधि-त्रयमेकत्र संयमः । एकविषयाणि त्रीणि साधनानि संयम इत्युच्यते । तदस्य त्रयस्य तान्त्रिकी परिभाषा संयम इति । तदप्यन्तरङ्गं साधनत्रयं निर्बीजस्य योगस्य बहिरङ्गं भवति । कस्मात् । तदभावे भावात् ।. The राजमार्तण्ड explains the word समाधि as सम्यगाधीयते एकाग्रीक्रियते विक्षेपान् परिहृत्य मनो यत्र स समाधिः ।. The योगसुधाकर p. 118 (Kashi series, 1930) brings out the distinction between संप्रज्ञात and असंप्रज्ञात समाधि as follows: ब्रह्माकारमनोवृत्तिमात्रशेषोऽहंकृतिं विना । संप्रज्ञातः समाधिः स्याद्भावनाभ्यासप्रकर्षतः । इति । ... परवैराग्यपूर्वकनिरोधप्रयत्नेन तस्यापि निरोधे सर्ववृत्तिनिरोधाद्निर्बीजः समाधिर्भवति । तदुक्तम् । मनसो वृत्तिशून्यस्य ब्रह्माकारतया स्थितिः । याऽसंप्रज्ञातनामासौ समाधिरभिधीयते । इत्येष विभागो ज्ञेयः । योगसुधाकर of सदाशिवेन्द्रसरस्वती on यो. सू. III. 3.]]

[[P1450]]meditation is carried to [[&|a]] stage where the object contemplated upon alone appears and the meditator is not [[consolous|conscious]] of the idea of contemplation, because the object meditated upon entirely engulfs the meditator. The yogin becomes so much identified with the object meditated upon that he is not conscious that he is contemplating upon an object. This is what is meant by [['svarupasunyam-iva'|'svarūpaśūnyam-iva']] (in Y. S. III. 3). In [[Samadhi|Samādhi]] the subject and object, the individual and Paramātman are completely blended into one, so that the consciousness of the subject as separate from object disappears. The word [['Samadhi'|'Samādhi']] does not occur in any of the ancient [[Upanigads,|Upaniṣads,]] though it is known to the [[Maitrayani|Maitrāyaṇī]] Up. (vide n. 2327 above). The word [[Samadhi|Samādhi]] occurs in the [[Bhagavadgita|Bhagavadgītā]] (II. 53-54) and in Vanaparva (3. 11) and in [[Santiparva 195. 19-20, Cb. ed.). | The|Śāntiparva 195. 19-20, Ch. ed.). The]] [[Viṣṇu. purapa2384|Viṣṇupurāṇa]][^2384] provides that it is called [[Samadhi|Samādhi]] when the mind grasps as a result of dhyāna the real nature of that (the Paramātman) in which there is absence of the separate apprehension (of the object to be meditated upon, the act of meditation and the meditator). The three (dhāraṇā, dhyāna and [[samadhi)|samādhi)]] are direct aids in samprajñāta-samādhi but they are indirect aids in [[asamprajñātasamādbi,|asamprajñātasamādhi,]] as this last may follow without these. The [[Hathayoga-pradipikā 2385|Hathayoga-pradīpikā]][^2385] states [['that|that]] is called [[Samadhi|Samādhi]] where there is equality and oneness of the individual self and the Highest Self and where all desires cease to [[exist'. Sabija and nirbija samadhis|exist'. Sabīja and nirbīja samādhis]] would be equal to savikalpa and nirvikalpa samādhi defined by the [[Vedantasāra.|Vedāntasāra.]] There are [[soveral|several]] grades or stages in [[samadhi.|samādhi.]] There are four [[grades 2385|grades]][^2386] of [[Samprajnata|Samprajñāta]]

[^2384]: तस्यैव कल्पनाहीनं स्वरूपग्रहणं हि यत् । मनसा [[ध्याननिष्पाचं|ध्याननिष्पाद्यं]] समाधिः सोऽभिधीयते । विष्णुपु. VI. 7. 92 q. by वाचस्पति, [[कुस्यकल्प|कृत्यकल्प०]] (मोक्ष p. 175), अपरार्क p. 1026 (who explains तस्य ब्रह्मणः कल्पनाहीनं [[पेयं ध्यानं यातेति भेदपल्यपरहित|ध्येयं ध्यानं ध्यातेति भेदकल्पनापररहितं]] etc.). The [[लिपु.|लिङ्गपु.]] I. 8. 44 is [['चिदभासमर्थमात्रस्य देहयमिव स्थितम् । समाधिः सर्वहतश्च प्राणायाम इति स्थितः"|'चित्प्रकाशमर्थमात्रस्य देहशून्यमिव स्थितम् । समाधिः सर्वहृत्स्थश्च प्राणायाम इति स्थितः ॥']]

[^2385]: [[तस्समं|तत्समं]] च द्वयोरेक्यं जीवात्मपरमात्मनोः । [[मनष्टसर्वसंकल्पः|प्रनष्टसर्वसंकल्पः]] समाधिः सोऽभिधीयते। [[३. यो.प्र.|ह. यो. प्र.]] IV. 7. This is practically the same as स्कन्द, काशी० 47. 127 [[पल्स म इयोन... निधीयते ।|यत्समं द्वयोश्च ... निधीयते ॥]]

[^2386]: तत्र शब्दार्थज्ञानविकल्पैः संकीर्णा सवितर्का [[समापतिः।|समापत्तिः ।]] स्मृतिपरिशुद्धौ स्वरूप[[अन्येवार्थमात्रनिर्भासानिविता।|शून्येवार्थमात्रनिर्भासा निर्वितर्का ।]] [[एतमेव|एतयैव]] च सविचारा [[निविचाराचक्ष्मविषया|निर्विचारा च सूक्ष्मविषया]] [[व्याख्याता।... सा एवं सीजः|व्याख्याता । ... ता एव सबीजः]] समाधिः । [[यो..|यो. सू.]] I. [[42-44,463;|42-44, 46;]] तद्यथा गौरिति शब्दो [[गोरिस्पों|गौरित्यर्थो]] गौरिति ज्ञानमित्यविभागेन विभक्तानामपि ग्रहणं [[रहम् ।|दृष्टम् ।]] [[विभाज्यमानाचाम्ये शवपर्मा श्रम्पेऽधर्मा भने|विभाज्यमानाश्चान्ये शब्दधर्मा अन्येऽर्थधर्मा अन्ये]] विज्ञानधर्मा इत्येषां [[विभक्का पन्या|विभक्तः पन्थाः ।]] भाष्य.

[[P1451]]samādhi, viz. [[Bavitarka, pirritarka,|savitarka, nirvitarka,]] savicāra, [[nirvloāra.|nirvicāra.]] Vide p. 1411 note 2318 above. The word gauḥ, the object cow denoted by it and the [[oonoopt|concept]] or idea [[( jõāna)|(jñāna)]] 'this is a cow' are all really three separate matters, but they are apprehended as mixed up. When a yogin concentrates upon a gross object and his intellect is conscious of that object [[28|as]] permeated by all the above three, then that samādhi is savitarka [[(Y. S. I, 42).|(Y. S. I. 42).]] For the others vide note 2318 and below. In the asamprajñāta Samādhi, the ultimate reality [[dawas|dawns]] on the yogin, [[praksti|prakṛti]] does not affect him in any way, his soul which abides in itself and is not conscious of personality and even of joy is all cit or [[citeakti|citiśakti]] and nothing else. In this section about the influence of yoga on Dharmaśāstra it is not necessary to go deeply into the several stages of [[samadhi|samādhi]] described in the yoga works. The [[Gorakṣaśataka 2387|Gorakṣaśataka]][^2387] describes the final stage of samādhi as follows: 'A yogin in Samādhi does not apprehend smell, flavour, form, touch or sound nor does he apprehend [[him self|himself]] or others [[( as distinct);|(as distinct);]] [[kaowers|knowers]] of brahma hold it to be pure, immovable, eternal, not engaged in activities, free from the guṇas, expansive like the sky, as intelligence and bliss; the knower of yoga [[teaches|reaches]] non-duality in the highest stage as milk poured in milk, ghee into ghee or fire into fire.'

It would have been noted that in [[dharānā, dhyāna and samadhi|dhāraṇā, dhyāna and samādhi]] the principal emphasis is mental. External conditions do help in the training but they are subsidiary. As noted above [[(pp. 1421–22 )|(pp. 1421–22)]] cleanliness, contentment, austerities (tapas), sexual purity, certain easy postures and living in detachment, restrictions as to quantity and quality of food—these are the chief physical or external conditions. While the yogin practises the above three he may develop certain [[super-Dormal|super-normal]] powers which he is advised to ignore, as they are hindrances in the attainment of his goal (Yogasūtra III. 36). Though this is the opinion of [[Patanjali,|Patañjali,]] from the fact that with most yogins the siddhis are an important part of yoga doctrines and the fact that, out of 195 sūtras of the [[Yogasutra, 35 (III. 16-59)|Yogasūtra, 35 (III. 16-50)]] are devoted

I. 15 तिलेषु तैलं ... वह्निः with दुग्धे क्षीरं &c.]]

[[P1452]]to the description of siddhis, the present author is constrained to [[gay|say]] that siddhis are an integral part of Yoga. The [[Vaikhānasa. smārtasutra|Vaikhānasa-smārtasūtra]] says that the yogin can vanish from people, can [[800|see]] distant objects and hear in spite of great distance.

It is unnecessary to dwell upon the results of all [[santyamas|saṁyamas]] mentioned in Y. S. pāda three. A few are cited by way of sample. By [[saiyama|saṁyama]] on the power of an elephant one [[socures|secures]] the strength of an elephant [[(111. 24),|(III. 24),]] by samyama on the sun there arises the knowledge of the [[soven|seven]] worlds [[(11I. 26 )|(III. 26)]] and by samyama on the moon, there arises knowledge of the [[arrange ment|arrangement]] of the stars (III. 27), by samyama on the navel cakra arises knowledge of the arrangement of the body (III. 29, viz. the three [[doṣcze, rāta, pilla,|doṣas, vāta, pitta,]] kapha and the seven dhātus viz. skin, blood, flesh, sinews, bones, marrow and [[somen).|semen).]] By samyama on the coarse[^2388] forms, the essential attributes (svarūpa), the subtle form (tanmātrās), the inherence (anvaya) and the [[pur. posiveness|purposiveness]] of the five elements results the conquest (or mastery over) elements and from this results the appearance of the [[per fections|perfections]] called [[anıma|aṇimā]] and others, perfection of body and [[non obstruction|non-obstruction]] (of the actions of the yogin by the qualities of the elements [[i. e,|i. e.]] the earth cannot prevent the yogin by its hardness from penetrating [[insid|inside]] the earth's rocks nor can fire burn him &c.). In IV. 1 Patañjali states that supernormal powers (siddhis) arise in five ways viz. by birth in certain[^2389] bodies

[[Pre|स्वरूप]] consists of [[aature|nature]] or attributes of the five elements and is explained as solidness (or [[bardoess)|hardness)]] of the earth, liquidity of water, heat of fire, mobility of the wind, [[all pervading Dature|all-pervading nature]] of ākāśa. The 4th rūpa of the elements viz. anvaya is the attributes of [[khyūti (cogaition),|khyāti (cognition),]] activity and inertia. [[1904 explaios: 37vat Juni: 47319hfufaa

Ernest Wood on 'Yoga' (1959, in Penguin Books) observes on p. 104 about levitation [[(laghima)|(laghimā)]] 'I remember one occasion when an old [[yogio|yogin]] was levitated in [[8|a]] recumbent position about six feet above ground in an open field for about half an hour, while the visitors were permitted to pass sticks to and fro between'. He further refers to levitation which a princess of Sikkim asserted she saw as a girl. A. Koestler in 'The Lotus and the Robot' (London, 1960, on p. 114) doubts the quality of the evidence of E. Wood on the ground that no exact date or place is mentioned and asserts that no experiment on levitation with definite date and place has been published. But Dr. Alexander Cannon in 'The invisible influence' (1935) pp. 39-41 narrated a personal experience of [[Javitation (lagbimā).|levitation (laghimā).]] It is not clear whether A. Koestler was aware of this statement.

[[P1453]](e. g. being born as a bird which can fly high in the [[sky ),|sky),]] by the [[UBO|use]] of certain drugs, by the japa of certain mantras, by tapas (which is one of the niyamas) and by [[sanādhi,|samādhi,]] each [[succeed. ing|succeeding]] one being [[suporior|superior]] to each preceding one.

The siddhis have been enumerated and explained above on pp. 1112-13. The [[Davaladharmasūtra|Devaladharmasūtra]] has a long note on siddhis, which is quoted in Kalpataru [[(Moksakāṇda|(Mokṣakāṇḍa]] pp. 216-217).[^2390] The Yājñavalkya [[smrti|smṛti]] (III. 202-203) enumerates some [[charac teristio|characteristic]] signs of Yogasiddhi, viz. becoming invisible to others, remembrance of incidents in past lives, charming appearance, ability to see past and future happenings or objects that are distant, knowing what is being said at a great distance, entering another body after leaving one's own body, creation of things at one's will (without possessing the appropriate [[materials ).|materials).]]

A great deal has been said above on mantras under Tantra (pp. 1096-1107 and notes [[thereto ).|thereto).]] There are two theories about [[mantras ,|mantras;]] one is the vibration theory, viz. that the letters of the mantra are charged with certain powers by the original composer and user of it and when the mantra is repeated certain imperceptible vibrations are set up which help in securing the purpose for which the mantra is repeated. The other theory is that the mantra comes down from antiquity and from

[^2390]: A short extract from the long prose passage of [[Devala-dharma sūtra|Devala-dharmasūtra]] may be set out here. [[and 3: I HOAT ATEAT STEHT VUA: Mireitai वशित्वं यत्रकामावसायित्वं चाटावेश्वर्यगुणाः। तेषामणिमा महिमा लधिमा त्रयः शारीरा प्रापया दयः पञ्चेन्द्रियाः। ... शरीराशुगामित्वं लधिमा । तेनातिदूरस्थानपि क्षणेनासादयति। विश्वविषया. वाप्तिः प्राप्तिः। प्राप्त्या सर्वप्रत्यक्षदर्शी भवति । ... अमतिहतैश्वर्यनीशित्वम् । ईशित्वेन देवता नाप्यतिशेते। ... यत्रकामावसायित्वं|तत्र देवलः । अणिमा महिमा लघिमा प्राप्तिः प्राकाम्यमीशित्वं वशित्वं यत्रकामावसायित्वं चाष्टावैश्वर्यगुणाः । तेषामणिमा महिमा लघिमा त्रयः शारीराः । प्राप्यादयः पञ्चेन्द्रियाः । ... शरीराशुगामित्वं लघिमा । तेनातिदूरस्थानपि क्षणेनासादयति । विश्वविषयावाप्तिः प्राप्तिः । प्राप्त्या सर्वप्रत्यक्षदर्शी भवति । ... अप्रतिहतैश्वर्यमीशित्वम् । ईशित्वेन देवतानामप्यतिशेते । ... यत्रकामावसायित्वं]] त्रिविधम् — छायावेशः, अवध्यानावेशः, अनुप्रवेश इति । यत् परस्य अङ्गप्रवेशमात्रेण चित्तं वशीकरोति स छायावेशः । यद्दूरस्थानामपि अनुध्यानेन चित्ताधिष्ठानं सोऽवध्यानावेशः । [[यत्सजीवस्थोन्मिस्ते (?) जीवस्य वा शरीराद्यनुप्रवेशनं सोऽनुप्रवेशः । अन्तर्धानं स्मृतिः कान्तिर्बुद्धिः श्रोत्रज्ञता तथा । निजं शरीरमुत्सृज्य परकायाप्रवेशनम् । अर्थानां छन्दतः सृष्टिर्योगसिद्धेस्तु लक्षणम् ॥ या. III. 202-203. |यत्सजीवस्थोन्मिस्ते (?) जीवस्य वा शरीराद्यनुप्रवेशनं सोऽनुप्रवेशः । अन्तर्धानं स्मृतिः कान्तिर्बुद्धिः श्रोत्रज्ञता तथा । निजं शरीरमुत्सृज्य परकायाप्रवेशनम् । अर्थानां छन्दतः सृष्टिर्योगसिद्धेस्तु लक्षणम् ॥ या. [[P1454]]a great sage and its power of suggestion is great. But the real effective power of a mantra seems to the present author to depend far more on the knowledge, the responsiveness and the high spiritual level which the person who repeats it brings to bear on its use. No scientific tests have been employed and different works emphasize with exaggeration one of the two theories about mantras. All is speculation. The present author believes that the 2nd theory has greater elements of trust-worthiness than the first, as it relies on human psychology, while the first degenerated so much that the famous mantra 'om [[mani|maṇi]] padme hūm' (dedicated to Avalokiteśvara) written on some material and pasted on a wheel which was turned round and round hundreds of times was supposed to yield great results. The 2nd theory led to the importance of guru and [[dīksā|dīkṣā]] (initiation) and here also extravagant claims were made. This latter theory, however, required that the disciple should possess certain qualifications such as implicit faith in the guru, respect for matters spiritual, some knowledge of the tenets and the basic scriptures and readiness to devote time and energy to the study and practice of the teachings of the guru. Vide Śiva-saṁhitā (III. 10-19) for the relation between guru and disciple.

The fourth pāda deals with Kaivalya. The yogin who has gone through the whole discipline up to samādhi and has become thoroughly aware of the distinction between Puruṣa and the guṇas (sattva and others), becomes free from the influence of the three guṇas, because they merge in Pradhāna after having served the purpose of the soul, that is Kaivalya (isolation) or it (Kaivalya) may be stated to be the principle of consciousness that abides by itself (and does not become connected with even [[sattra-guna). 2391|sattva-guṇa).]][^2391] This has also been alluded to in Y. S. II. 25 that states that when avidyā (nescience) is removed by discrimination the self (who is the perceiver) does not come into contact with the guṇas, which state is [[Kaivalya, 2392|Kaivalya.]][^2392] Kaivalya

[^2391]: पुरुषार्थशून्यानां गुणानां प्रतिप्रसवः कैवल्यं स्वरूपप्रतिष्ठा वा चितिशक्तिरिति । यो. सू. IV. 34; भाष्य 'कृतभोगापवर्गाणां पुरुषार्थशून्यानां यः प्रतिप्रसवः कार्यकारणात्मकानां गुणानां तत्कैवल्यं, स्वरूपप्रतिष्ठा पुनर्बुद्धिसत्त्वानभिसम्बन्धात्पुरुषस्य चितिशक्तिरेव केवला, तस्याः सदा तथैवावस्थानं कैवल्यमिति' । वाचस्पति explains प्रतिप्रसवः as स्वकारणे प्रधाने लयः.

[^2392]: तस्य हेतुरविद्या । तदभावात्संयोगाभावो हानं तद्दृशेः कैवल्यम् । यो. सू. II. 24-25 ; तस्यादर्शनस्याभावाद् बुद्धेः पुरुषसंयोगाभाव आत्यन्तिको बन्धनोपरम इत्यर्थः । एतद्धानम् । तद्दृशेः पुरुषस्यामिश्रीभावः पुनरसंयोगो गुणैरित्यर्थः । दुःखकारणनिवृत्तौ दुःखोपरमो हानं तदा स्वरूपप्रतिष्ठः पुरुष इत्युक्तम् । भाष्य. कैवल्य means 'एकाकिता' remaining alone by itself.

[[P1455]]is put forward in IV. 34 from two points of view. When a puruṣa ceases to be affected in any way by the guṇas (that constitute prakṛti) because he has become entirely passionless, prakṛti becomes aloof (kevala) so far as that puruṣa is concerned. When puruṣa has thorough knowledge and ceases to be affected by guṇas he remains as bare consciousness (citiśakti) and remains aloof (kevala) and that is the 2nd point of view about kaivalya. In that state of kaivalya or mokṣa we cannot predicate any happiness or bliss about him but we can only say about him that he is in a state of bare consciousness (citiśakti). The Upaniṣads declare[^2393] that in such a state the liberated soul has neither joy nor sorrow, that happiness or its opposite does not touch such an one, since he has risen above identifying the body with himself. The ideal of Yoga is to be jīvan-mukta (i. e. to reject Life and Personality, to die to this world though the body may persist for some time).

The eight [[angas|aṅgas]] of Yoga are described in more or less detail in several Purāṇas. Vide Agni, chap. 214-215 and 372-76, Bhāgavata III. 28, Kūrma II. 11, Narasiṁha 61 (verses 3-13 of which are quoted by Kalpataru, mokṣakāṇḍa pp. 194-195), Matsya chap. 52, Mārkaṇḍeya (chap. 36-40 of Veṅk. edition, 39-43 of Cal. ed.) about 250 verses, many of which are quoted by Kṛtyakalpataru (on Mokṣa), Aparārka and others; Liṅga I. 8., Vāyu, chap. 10-15, Viṣṇu VI. 7 (which closely follows the Y. S. in thought and phraseology), Viṣṇudharmottara III. 280-284, Skanda ([[Kāsikhanda,|Kāśīkhaṇḍa,]] chap. 41).

Geraldine Coster in 'Yoga and Western Psychology' (Oxford Uni. Press, 1934) observes 'I am convinced that the ideas on which Yoga is based are universally true for mankind and that we have in the Yoga-sūtras a body of material which we could investigate and use with infinite advantage (p. 244)... My plea is then that Yoga as followed in the East is a practical method of mind development, quite as practical as analytical therapy and far more practical and closely related to real life than the average university course. I am convinced that the Yogasūtras of Patañjali do really contain the information [[2393|]]that some of the most advanced psychotherapists of the present day are ardently seeking' (p. 245).

[^2393]: [[starcie are free tranet yara: 1 31. 5'|अशरीरं वाव सन्तं न प्रियाप्रिये स्पृशतः । छा. उप. VIII. 12. 1; अध्यात्मयोगाधिगमेन देवं मत्वा धीरो हर्षशोकौ जहाति । कठ. II. 12. वे. सू. IV. 4. 2 (मुक्तः प्रतिज्ञानात्) is based on छा. उप. VIII. 12. 1.]]

[[P1456]]The last chapter in Dr. Behanan's book on 'Yoga, a scientific evaluation' is interesting and important. He makes an appraisal of several aspects of Yoga by means of experiments which he carried out on himself after spending one year under the guidance of Swami Kuvalayānand of Lonavla and after he had three years of experience in Yogic breathing. It is not possible for reasons of space to give here a summary of his appraisal of several aspects of Yoga but reference may be made to a few of his findings. He finds that by yogic practices the mind is turned inward and detached from the external world (p. 232), it seems to him very probable that the prāṇāyāmic breathing induces a state of relaxation, thereby inducing the mind to take an inward course (p. 234), that taking normal breathing as a basis of comparison it was found that oxygen consumption increased 24. 5 per cent in [[Ujjāyi,|Ujjāyī,]] 18. 5 per cent in bhastrikā and 12 per cent in Kapālabhāti; that the nasal gaze in a meditative posture is an aid to checking the wandering propensities of the mind (p. 242), that Yogic practices lead to an emotional stability and that watching at close range for a year the daily lives of more than half a dozen practitioners of yoga Dr. Behanan concluded that they were the happiest personalities that he had known and that their serenity was contagious (p. 245).

Dr. P. A. Sorokin of the Harvard University, one of the greatest living sociologists, contributes a very valuable paper on 'Yoga and man's transfiguration' to the Bhāratīya Vidyā Bhavan's Journal for November 1958 (pp. 111-120), the very first sentence of which opens with the words 'The methods and techniques of the yoga, particularly those of the [[Rajayoga,|Rājayoga,]] contain in themselves nearly all the sound techniques of modern psycho-analysis, psychotherapy, psycho-drama, moral education, and education of character.'

That a person engaged in Yoga practices is succeeding in reaching higher and higher spiritual levels is shown by the appearance of certain traits in himself. The [[Sv. Up.|Śv. Up.]] states that the first favourable signs of the working of Yoga are: lightness or agility of body, health, freedom from desires, brightness of complexion, fineness of voice, pleasant bodily odour, passing only a small quantity of urine or excreta. Almost the same verse occurs in the [[Vāyu 2394|Vāyu]][^2394] and the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇas and

[^2394]: [[लघुत्वमारोग्यमलोलुपत्वं वर्णप्रसादः स्वरसौष्ठवं च । गन्धः शुभो मूत्रपुरीषमल्पं योगप्रवृत्तिं प्रथमां वदन्ति ॥ श्वेता. उप. 11. 11; वायु. 11. 63 reads सत्त्वं तथारोग्य ...|लघुत्वमारोग्यमलोलुपत्वं वर्णप्रसादः स्वरसौष्ठवं च । गन्धः शुभो मूत्रपुरीषमल्पं योगप्रवृत्तिं प्रथमां वदन्ति ॥ श्वेता. उप. II. 11; वायु. 11. 63 reads सत्त्वं तथारोग्य ... वर्णप्रभा सुस्वरसौम्यता च गन्ध ... योगप्रवृत्तिः प्रथमा शरीरे ॥. The मार्कण्डेय reads अलौल्यमारोग्यमनिष्ठुरत्वं गन्धः ... ल्पम् । कान्तिः प्रसादः स्वरसौम्यता च योगप्रवृत्तेः प्रथमे हि चिह्ने ॥ 36. 63 (= 39. 63 in Cal. ed.). This and two more verses of Mārkaṇḍeya (36. 64-65) are q. by कृत्यकल्प० (on मोक्ष) p. 211.]]

[[P1457]][[Mārkandeya|Mārkaṇḍeya]] further says [['people come'|'people come]] to like the yogin and sing praises of his qualities behind his back and all animals are not afraid of him; he is not affected by severe cold or heat and is not afraid of others; these show that siddhi in Yoga is approaching.' The Vāyupurāṇa adds 'if the practitioner of Yoga sees the earth or his body as if on fire and if he sees himself entering the elements (or all beings) he should understand that success in Yoga is [[near.' 2395|near.']][^2395]

The [[Mārkandeya-purāṇa|Mārkaṇḍeya-purāṇa]] (chap. 38 verse 26) and the [[Visnupurāna|Viṣṇupurāṇa]] II. 13 describe at some length [[yogicaryā (i e.|yogicaryā (i. e.]] behaviour or conduct of a yogin). It is not possible to set out all that is said in these chapters but two striking verses are rendered here. The [[Mārkandeya 2396|Mārkaṇḍeya]][^2396] says 'honour and disrespect cause pleasure and distress to man (in general), but these two have the reverse effect and serve to produce perfection in the yogin; these two are called poison and nectar respectively; disrespect is nectar for the yogin and honour is terrible poison.' The [[Visnupurāna|Viṣṇupurāṇa]] emphasizes that a yogin should so act that people will disrespect him and would not seek his company. The [[Manusmrti|Manusmṛti]] (in VI. 38-85) enlarges upon the duties of [[sannyssins,|sannyāsins,]] many of which are applicable to yogins. In VI. 65 Manu calls upon the sannyāsin to ponder by means of Yoga over the subtleness of the Highest Self and in VI. 73 [[Many advises the asootio|Manu advises the ascetic]] to practise dhyānayoga. The [[Yājñavalkyasmīti ako|Yājñavalkyasmṛti also]] (in III. 56-67) prescribes the duties of sannyāsins, one of which (in [[Verse 64)|verse 64)]] is the same as Manu VI. 73.

The Śāntiparva [[provides 2397|provides]][^2397] that a man is said to be a yogin by those [[wło|who]] know the procedure and [[ordinanons of You|ordinances of Yoga,]]

[^2395]: [[The groft sa u ulei restante e forma guretat a erg 11. 64 g. by Heyeting (oa ATE) B. 211.|आत्मानं पृथिवीं चैव ज्वलन्तीं यदि पश्यति । भूतान्यन्तर्विशते चैव विद्यात्सिद्धिसुपस्थिताम् ॥ वायु 11. 64 q. by कृत्यकल्प० (on मोक्ष) p. 211.]]
